The decree also mentions that immigration authorities should issue\nresidence permits directly without a preceding visa procedure for persons who are already in Germany in\ncases where a return to Russia would put applicants in danger.824 For persons who do not fulfil the criteria\nfor a residence title in Germany or for being granted international protection, PRO ASYL assumes that\nthey should be issued a tolerated stay (Duldung) on the basis that removals to Russia are currently\nimpossible.825 Despite the decree in 2022 to grant special rights to Russian cultural and media workers,\nRussian journalists who fled to Germany report that they often only received tolerated stay (Duldung),\nwhich forces them to stay in Germany without possibilities to secure their livelihood and to continue their\nwork as journalists.826\n\nIn the first half of 2023 no person was removed to Russia and no person with Russian nationality was\nremoved involuntarily from Germany.827\n\n      5. Palestinian territories\n\nThe attack by the Hamas on Israel on the 7 October 2023 and the following escalating conflict has led to\npolitical discussions and rifts in the public perception. Following the attack, chancellor Scholz declared\nthe security of Israel as a reason of state for Germany. He claimed that Germany’s place is on the side of\nIsrael and that Germany stands in full solidarity and supports Israel.828 The German government continues\nto position itself in favour for Israel, e.g., by its abstention to the UN resolution and its rejection of an EU\nresolution on ceasefire. Only very wary and situational criticism to the Israelian government and its\nreaction to the attack by the German government have been voiced by the German government. At a\npress conference of the Foreigners Office on 9 October 2023, the speaker of the Foreigners Office\nmentioned that in the past the German government has voiced criticism to the Israelian handling of the\nmiddle-east conflict and generally envisages a two-state solution but that at the current moment the focus\nshould be the support of Israel’s defence.829 Since then, the Minister of Foreign Affairs increasingly raises\ncriticism to specific actions of the Israelian government, e.g., calling for humanitarian corridors and\nsupport, demanding the protection of civil society and the adherence to International Humanitarian Law.830\nNevertheless, the German government has announced to support Israel in the case pending before the\nInternational Criminal Court.831 The public perception is rifted. In the aftermaths of the attack, an increase\n\n823\n        Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, Beschäftigung von regimekritischen Kultur- und\n        Medienschaffenden aus der Russischen Föderation in Deutschland; At the same time, it has been reported that pro-Palestinian demonstrations\nhave been prohibited per se without any distinction to the cause they were protesting for, which amounted\nto a violation of the equal freedom of assembly. Additionally, the police have been criticised for its brutal\nreactions against pro-Palestinian demonstrations being in parts racially motivated.832 Some associations\nlike Jews and Palestinians for Peace and Combatants for Peace try to lead the public debate back to the\nfacts and a constructive exchange in providing information and workshops for schools and other\nassociations.833\n\nWhat impact the situation in the Palestinian territories and the political climate here in Germany have on\nPalestinian refugees in Germany is currently difficult to evaluate. The number of 743 asylum applicants\nin 2023 from Palestinian territories has significantly increased compared to 35 in 2022.834 This could be a\nresult of the escalating conflict. Another contributing factor to the increase of asylum applicants might\nhave been the court rulings of the Administrative Court in Oldenburg and of the Court of Justice of the\nEU. The Administrative Court in Oldenburg decided already in June 2023, prior to the escalation, that the\ncurrent situation in the West Bank amounts to a danger to the health and life of those living there and that\ntherefore persons present in Germany are eligible for toleration (‘Duldung’) under national law.835 The\nEuropean Court of Human Rights affirmed that UNRWA does no longer guarantee protection for\nPalestinians, making them eligible for national protection.836 Looking at the political debate, it seems that\nthere is a harsh climate not only with regard to the middle-east conflict but also vis-à-vis Palestinian\nrefugees. The Christian Democrats (CDU) affirmed that while humanitarian aid will be provided, migration\nflows to Germany should be prevented. The Social Democrats stated that the right to claim asylum applies\nto everyone equally and that possible security threats are checked for Palestinians as for every other\nasylum applicant.837\n\n\n\n\n832\n      Clara Neumann, Das Spannungsverhältnis zwischen Staatsräson und Grundrechten, 8 December 2023,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/3St683X.\n833\n      Tagesschau.de, Die Hoffnung auf Frieden, 16 December 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3w1d0ye;\n      WDR.de, Jüdin und Palästinenserin: “Ohne Dialog geht es nicht”, 19 November 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3unv70F.\n834\n      BAMF, Asylgeschäftsstatistik (statistics on applications, decisions and pending procedures), 1-12/2023,\n      available at: htps://bit.ly/3UjFWf0 1-12/2022 available at: https://bit.ly/3Z7UIUM.\n835\n      Administrative Court Oldenburg, Decision 3 A 3611/21, 7 June 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3waRhDM.\n836\n      CJEU, Decision C-294/22, 5 October 2023, available at: https://bit.ly/49noIBC.\n837\n      Rheinische Post.de, Union will keine palästinensischen Flüchtlinge, 18 October 2023, available in Germany\n      at: https://bit.ly/3up1I6n.\n\n\n                                                     129", "excerpt_sha256": "a3f1ae3ed27b6025d732038e85d8ff095c238f162a8ab7f03fe8d321e96684a5", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R04-E013", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; Housing\n\n      1. Types of accommodation\n\n                                      Indicators: Types of Accommodation\n      1. Type of accommodation most frequently used in an accelerated procedure:\n            Reception centre  Hotel or hostel    Emergency shelter     Private housing                  Other\n\nIn general, 3 types of accommodation for asylum seekers can be distinguished:\n    v Initial reception centres, including particular types of centres such as arrival centres, special\n        reception centres and AnkER-centres;\n    v Collective accommodation centres;\n    v Decentralised accommodation.\n\nEmergency shelters were reintroduced in a greater scale in 2022, especially in bigger cities, following the\nrising numbers of protection seekers from Afghanistan and Ukraine (See also Annex on Temporary\nProtection). According to a survey from the University Heidelberg about the municipal accommodation of\nasylum seekers, approximately 45 % of German municipalities use emergency shelters.938 In Berlin the\nformer airport Tegel is used as emergency shelter and its capacities have been continuously expanded\nsince its reintroduction. In July 2022 tents located in the former Terminal A and B had a capacity for 900\nprotection seekers which were extended to 1,900 in October 2022.939 Whereas in the beginning the\nemergency shelters should only be provided until the end of 2022, the Berlin Senate decided that due to\nthe arrivals from Ukraine a prolongation is required until 15 March 2023.940 As all other reception centres\nin Berlin are completely full, the Senate has again prolonged the usage of the former airport until June\n2024. However, the issue of funding remains highly controversial. The Federal states are constantly\ndemanding more money, while the federal government believes it has already fulfilled its\nresponsibilities.944 In 2023, the Federal Government expected to support the municipalities with EUR 2,8\nbillion and plans a support of EUR 1,3 billion in 2024.945\n\n        1.1    Initial reception centres\n\nFollowing the reform of June 2019, asylum seekers are generally obliged to stay in an initial reception\ncentre for a period of up to 18 months after their application has been lodged (Aufnahmeeinrichtung).946\nAn obligation to stay in these centres for a maximum of 24 months can be imposed by Federal States\nsince July 2017 (see Freedom of movement).947 Furthermore, asylum seekers from safe countries of\norigin are obliged to stay there for the whole duration of their procedures.\n\nThe Federal States are required to establish and maintain the initial reception centres.948 Accordingly,\nthere is at least one such centre in each of Germany's 16 Federal States with most Federal States having\nseveral initial reception facilities.\n\nInitial reception centres are assigned to a branch office of the BAMF, or combined with a branch office to\nconstitute an arrival centre or AnkER centre. At the beginning of 2024, out of 58 branch offices listed on\nthe BAMF website 17 were integrated in arrival centres in 12 different Federal States, and nine were part\nof AnkER centres in three Federal States.949\n\nArrival centres\n\nSince 2016, several reception centres have either been opened as arrival centres (Ankunftszentren) or\nexisting facilities have been transformed into arrival centres. In these centres, the BAMF and other\nrelevant authorities are grouped together and apply fast-track processing. The concept of ‘arrival centres’\nis not established in law, therefore technically the initial reception centres are still functioning as part of\nthe arrival centres, together with a branch office of the BAMF and other relevant authorities. As of January\n2024, the BAMF lists 17 arrival centres which are located across 12 Federal States (down from 22 in\n2018):950\n    v Berlin                                                    v North Rhine-Westphalia: Bielefeld,\n    v Bremen                                                         Bonn, Mönchengladbach, Unna\n    v Hamburg                                                   v Saxony: Chemnitz, Leipzig\n    v Baden-Württemberg: Heidelberg\n\n\n942\n      Zeit Online, Neue Unterkunft für 840 Geflüchtete soll Freitag öffnen, 19 December 2022, available in German\n      at: http://bit.ly/3XPPP2h.\n943\n      NDR.de, Erste Flüchtlinge kommen in den Hamburger Messehallen unter, 16 October 2023 available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/3Uk2s79; WDR.de, NRW eröffnet Notunterkunft für Flüchtlinge in Kölner Messe, 17\n      November 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3SDw7WP.\n944\n      Tagesschau.de, Mit nackten Zahlen gegen die Länder, 03 May 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/49wE6f9.\n945\n      Unterrichtung durch die Bundesregierung, Finanzplan des Bundes 2023 bis 2027, 18 August 2023, available\n      in German at: https://bit.ly/3vN1PsD.\n946\n      Section 47(1) Asylum Act.\n947\n      Section 47(1b) Asylum Act.\n948\n      Section 44(1) Asylum Act.\n949\n      BAMF, Locations, available at: https://bit.ly/3dFTd8w. Some\n      branch offices also have several locations, which are not included in the count.\n950\n      BAMF, Locations, available at: https://bit.ly/2Z74Uko.\n\n\n                                                     146", "excerpt_sha256": "a11ac4c3cd08ba878d008bd3ba33d3743bdcd3e58001d8d94c24bc0f2fea8325", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R04-E014", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; 2023 Update", "publisher": "ECRE / AIDA", "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "In 2023 reception capacity was severely strained: Berlin and Hamburg were around 97% occupied, Berlin required 8,000 additional places, and overcrowded facilities produced reports of cold tents, illness, inadequate sanitation, lack of privacy, registration backlogs, insufficient health care, and violent conflicts; the federal government and states agreed on additional federal aid in November 2023.", "question_ids": ["3B.6", "3B.7", "3B.9", "5B.6", "5B.9", "6B.2", "6B.3", "6B.6", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8", "8B.9"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "The capacity shortages, reported conditions, and November 2023 federal-state agreement concern 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "The report attributes the funding agreement to the federal government and federal states, while accommodation operations are primarily local and state responsibilities.", "limitations": ["The cited harms are drawn from multiple local reports and are not presented as a nationally standardized inspection result."], "start_unit": 151, "end_unit": 153, "locator": "page 151 through page 153", "exact_excerpt": "regulations provide for 6 or 7m² per person.971 A typical room in an initial reception centre has between 2\nand 4 beds, there are chairs and a table and each resident has a locker for themselves. Size of rooms\nmay vary, but rooms with a single bed are highly exceptional.\n\nMost initial reception centres have a policy to accommodate single women and families in separate\nbuildings or separate wings of their buildings. The AnKER centre in Manching/Ingolstadt for example\nprovides separate rooms for vulnerable persons.\n\nBath and toilet facilities usually consist of shower rooms and toilets which people have to share. As\nmentioned before, there are no regulations that oblige the Federal states to fulfil any standards. However,\nthe Federal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth and UNICEF have worked out\na policy paper stating minimum standards concerning the protection of refugees in reception centres.972\nThese guidelines state that sanitary facilities should be separate between genders and that those facilities\nshould be built in a way that guarantees privacy. Other guidelines recommend that one shower should be\navailable for 10 to 12 persons, but in some reception centres the ratio is worse than that, particularly in\nsituations of overcrowding. Cleaning of shared space (halls, corridors) as well as of sanitary facilities is\ncarried out by external companies in the initial reception centres.\n\nFood is supplied in the initial reception centres and is usually served in canteens on the premises of the\ncentres. In general, two or more menus are on offer for lunch and the management of the catering facilities\ntries to ensure that specific food is provided with regard to religious sentiments. Some, but not all initial\nreception centres also have shared kitchen space which enables asylum seekers to cook their own food;\nin AnkER centres, for instance, cooking is not allowed. Refrigerators for the use of asylum seekers are\navailable in some initial reception centres, but this seems to be the exception. In some centres, the\nmanagement does not allow hot water boilers for asylum seekers as this would be forbidden by fire\nregulations.973\n\nThe living conditions in many initial reception centres have been criticised by asylum seekers, volunteers\nand NGOs – especially in light of the extended obligatory stay in these facilities. In 2022 the conditions\ndeteriorated even more due to the massive overcrowding as consequence from the war in Ukraine and\nthe situation in Afghanistan. But still in 2023, small cities like Pulheim in North Rhine-Westphalia,974 as\nwell as middle size municipalities like Augsburg in Bavaria975 and Aachen in North Rhine-Westphalia976\nbut also big municipalities like Berlin977 and Hamburg978 face difficulties in accommodating new\nprotection seekers. In Berlin and Hamburg, around 97% percent of the reception capacities were\noccupied around October and November 2023.979\n\n\n\n\n971\n      European Migration Network, The Organisation of Reception Facilities for Asylum Seekers in Germany, 2013,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/48ojgO9, 26.\n972\n      Federal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth and UNICEF, Mindeststandards\n      zum Schutz von geflüchteten Menschen in Flüchtlingsunterkünften, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/48WAaV2.\n973\n      PROASYL and Refugee Council Berlin, Das Asylbewerberleistungsgesetz – Einschränkungen des\n      Grundrechts auf ein menschenwürdiges Existenzminimum für Geflüchtete, November 2022, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/3XrdSox, 100.\n974\n       WDR.de, Geflüchtete klagen über menschenunwürdige Zustände in Pulheimer Flüchtlingsunterkünften,\n      available at: https://bit.ly/3SKjme1.\n975\n      Augsburger Allgemeine, Augsburg gerät bei der Unterbringung von Geflüchteten wieder stärker unter Druck,\n      19 August 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/42MZ1Iq.\n976\n      InfoMigrants, ‘Refugees: German cities are reaching their limits’, 26 September 2022, available at:\n      https://bit.ly/3wpxjCp.\n977\n      Silke Mehring, Leichtbauhallen in Tegel geplant Unterbringung von Ukraine-Flüchtlingen in Berlin wird immer\n      komplizierter, rbb24, 02 December.2022, available in German at: http://bit.ly/3wdFPUT.\n978\n      Ibid.\n979\n      Rbb24.de, Berlin droht Verlust von tausenden Unterkunftsplätzen für Geflüchtete, 28 Novermber 2023,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/48nVWzN; NDR, Flüchtlingszahlen: Senat sieht Hamburg am Limit, 6\n      October 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3SHfJ7w.\n\n\n                                                     151\n\nAccording to the administration of Berlin, 10,000 additional places were required in 2022.980 At the end\nof September 2023, the senate administration of Berlin spoke again of 8,000 more required places until\nthe end of 2023.981 Those necessary places are to be created in gymnasiums, hotels, exhibitions halls\nand through further usage of the old airport Tegel. In October 2023, the initial reception centre in Suhl,\nThuringia, was completely full and had imposed a freeze on admissions.982 However, the municipalities\ncannot offer any other accommodation facilities as all centres and apartments are full. The only possibility\nleft, according to the Thuringian Association of Towns and Municipalities, is the usage of sport halls.983\nMunicipalities in North Rine-Westphalia have started to rent hotels to provide accommodation for asylum\nseekers.984 Although this practice is very costly, it is preferred over blocking gymnasiums of local sport\nassociations. The authorities on the local, state and federal level blame each other for the shortcomings.\nWhile the local authorities are by law responsible for the accommodation of protection seekers,985 they\nclaim that the do not have enough financial and housing resources to fulfil the current need. They therefore\nask the Federal States to vacate more housing properties.986 The Federal States in turn urge the Federal\ngovernment to strengthen their efforts and to take up a coordinating role.987 After months of conflict, the\nFederal government and the Federal States have agreed in November 2023 – next to some deterrent\nmeasures that should decrease migration – upon more financial aid by the Federal Government.988\n\nAs consequence of the overcrowding, the authorities seem to be overburdened and deteriorating\nconditions have been reported. Under the law the state may derogate from the obligation to stay in initial\nreception centres in cases of overcrowding.989 Nevertheless, so far only Berlin has used this derogation\nclause and allows asylum seekers who have been allocated to Berlin under the “Köngisteiner Schlüssel”\nto live in private accommodations since the end of January 2023.990 In emergency shelters e.g. in Berlin,\nit has been reported that the tents at the former Berlin-Tegel airport do not protect from the cold causing\nnumerous illnesses and facilitating the spread of Covid-19 (on conditions in reception facilities during the\nCovid-19 pandemic see the 2021 Update).991 One partitioned area on such a tent is in general shared by\neight people. As a result, each asylum seeker has, according to calculations by the refugee council Berlin,\nonly about 2,6 m2 available for themselves.992 Additionally, since the airport is surrounded by barbed wire\nno systematic access for NGOs and volunteers is granted. At the same time protection seekers need to\ntake a shuttle bus to enter and exit the emergency shelter, thereby making it difficult for protection seekers\nto access legal aid and social assistance. In the emergency tents in Bremen protection seekers report of\nnon-functional and unclean sanitary facilities, coldness due to non-functional heating systems and a tense\natmosphere.993 Only one month later the municipality of Bremen decided to evacuate the tents due to the\n\n980\n      Thomas Frey, Ukraine-Ankunftszentrum wird um 3200 Plätze erweitert, Berliner Woche 06 January 2023,\n      available in German at: http://bit.ly/3CY9Jjy.\n981\n      Senatskanzlei Berlin, Mehr Plätze für die Unterbringung von Geflüchteten, press release from 26 September\n      2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3wnKSpd.\n982\n      Mdr.de, Erstaufnahmeeinrichtungen für Flüchtlinge – Was tun, wenn sie voll sind?, 6 October 2023, available\n      in German at: https://bit.ly/3I9iV74.\n983\n      Ibid.\n984\n      Wdr.de, Flüchtlinge in Kommunen: Letzter Ausweg Hotel, 26 January 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3UPlLFY.\n985\n      §44 AsylG in conjunction with the different Federal state’s Reception laws: e.g. §1 Landesaufnahmegesetz\n      Hesse; Inhabitants were partially relocated to emergency shelters on\nexhibition grounds. This solution was only temporary and local authorities have organised lightweight\nbuilding constructions which should serve until mid-2024.994 Even if asylum seekers do not live in tents\nbut in houses, the living conditions are in many cases catastrophic. In a reception centre in Pulheim,\nNorth Rhine-Westphalia, for example, the building smells heavily, the sanitary facilities are mouldy and\nrats run around the complex.995\n\nIn arrival centres, the overcrowding mostly leads to backlogs in the registration procedure and conflicts\namong the protection seekers stemming from the lack of privacy. Asylum seekers at the arrival centre in\nHamburg-Rahlstedt, for example, have reported inter alia a backlog of registration,996 lack of privacy,\nunclean sanitary facilities and disturbances at night. The sleeping areas are placed in former warehouses\nand divided by thin partitions into several compartments, which do not allow for privacy. Besides reading\nlamps attached to each bed, there is one common light for the whole warehouse, which is switched on\nfrom 8:00am to 22:00pm.997 A backlog of registration, lack of access to health care and social assistance\nhas been reported also from the arrival centre in Berlin.998 In the arrival centre in Thuringia, many violent\nconflicts have been reported stemming from the lack of staff members, stressed social workers and non-\ntrained security personnel.999 The backlog of registrations all over Germany has supposedly been caught\nup until the end of June 2023, according to the Federal Government.1000\n\nMore generally, studies published in 2020 have come to the conclusion that the accommodation in initial\nreception centres infringes upon children’s’ rights and constitutes a danger to their mental health. The\nspatial confinement, the experience of violence and removals, as well as the permanent uncertainty cause\npsychological stress and have a negative impact on children.1001 Health care and psychosocial support\nprovided for young refugees in the mass accommodations were described as worryingly inadequate for\nmost of the facilities.1002 The study of PROASYL and the Refugee Council Berlin support these findings.\nAccording to the study of 2022, especially the access to health care, access to adequate hygienic and\nother products such as strollers for toddlers are scarce.1003\n\nThe NGO ‘Ärzte der Welt’ (Doctors of the World) announced in September 2019 that an advice service\nrun by the organisation in the AnkER-centre of Manching/Ingolstadt was to be terminated. The NGO\ndescribed living conditions in the facility as ‘morbid’ and claimed that adequate treatment, in particular\ntreatment of persons with psychological disorders, was impossible under the circumstances. Insufficient\nprotection against assaults, lack of privacy and nocturnal disturbances were impeding the mental\nstabilisation of asylum-seekers at the facility and the NGO was no longer capable to bear responsibility\nfor the mental health of its patients. Moreover, the organisation claims that there was no system for the\nidentification of vulnerable persons in place at the facility.1004\n\n994\n       Weserreport, Geflüchtete ziehen um, 10 January "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "In 2023, Germany carried out 16,430 removals but recorded 31,770 failed removal attempts; 15,798 cancellations were attributed to local authorities revoking removal orders and 14,011 to local authorities failing to hand people over to the Federal Police, while only 295 cases cited resistance by the person being removed.", "question_ids": ["6B.7", "8B.5", "8B.7", "8B.8"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "The quantitative results cover the full 2023 calendar year.", "ruler_attribution": "The federal government established the return-enforcement objective and reported the figures, but most failed-removal causes identified here occurred at local-authority level.", "limitations": ["A failed attempt does not necessarily mean that the person was never subsequently removed.", "The source says the precise circumstances behind many local-authority cancellations were unclear."], "start_unit": 175, "end_unit": 176, "locator": "page 175 through page 176", "exact_excerpt": "Legal changes as a result of the ‘enforcement deficit’ debate\n\nDespite the stable number of removals over the last years prior to the Covid-19 outbreak, an alleged\n‘enforcement deficit’ had become the subject of a heated political debate and a ‘media obsession’ in 2017\nand 2018, as the authorities were being criticised for their failure to carry out removals.1158 This debate\nhas continued under the government that took office in 2021, which pledged to increase efforts to enforce\nreturns.1159 The debate has led to numerous restrictive reforms in 2017, 2019, 2020 and 2022 as well to\nan additional reform proposed in November 2023 (see below), and to a demand for increased use of\ndetention in the removal procedure. In 2023, a total of 31,770 removals failed, compared to 16,430\neffective removals.1160 This does not mean that all 31,770 persons were not returned, however, since\nauthorities often carry out another removal attempt after the failed one.1161 The reasons for failure to carry\nout removals were as follows:\n\n                               Failed removals in focus: 2023 (whole year)\n        Reasons for cancellation or abandonment of removal measures                        Number of cases\nRevocation of removal order by local authorities (before persons to be deported\n                                                                                                 15,798\nwere handed over to the Federal Police)\nFailure by local authorities to hand over persons to be deported to the Federal\n                                                                                                 14,011\nPolice (reasons unknown)\nResistance of persons to be deported                                                               295\nRefusal of pilots or other flight personnel to transport the person to be deported                 232\nRefusal of Federal Police or escort personnel to take reasons, strikes etc.)                                       93\nMedical concerns                                                                                    86\nLegal actions (appeals or interim measures)                                                         56\n(Attempted) suicides or self-harm                                                                   7\nAttempt to flee or abscond                                                                          2\nRefusal by receiving states to accept deported persons                                              13\nLack of travel documents                                                                            11\nLack of escort personnel      558\n\nSource: Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by the AFD 20/10520, 01 March 2024, available in\nGerman at: https://bit.ly/4aNCZIq, 16.\n\nThe above statistics show that in most cases, the reasons for the failure of removals can be found at the\nlevel of local authorities, although it is not clear which exact circumstances led to the cancellation of\n\n\n1158\n       Deutsche Welle, How do removals work in Germany?, 16 July 2018, available at: https://bit.ly/2Hhh3uM.\n1159\n       SPD, BÜNDNIS 90/DIE GRÜNEN UND FDP, ‚Mehr Fortschritt wagen. Bündnis für Freiheit, Gerechtigkeit und\n       Nachhaltigkeit. Koalitionsvertrag 2021 – 2025 zwischen der Sozialdemokratischen Partei Deutschlands (SPD)\n       und den Freien Demokraten (FDP)‘, 137-142, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3ITYqJZ.\n1160\n       DIE ZEIT, Zahl der Abschiebungen deutlich gestiegen, 17 January 2024, available in German at:\n       https://bit.ly/3uwVuBv.\n1161\n       PRO ASYL, Das angebliche »Abschiebungsvollzugsdefizit«: Statistisch fragwürdig, aber gut für Schlagzeilen,\n       14 July 2020, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3XLnGtl.\n\n\n                                                      175\n\nremoval measures in such cases. The Federal Government has no information on the number of cases\nin which persons to be deported were responsible for the cancellations (e.g. by absconding) and there\nare numerous other possible reasons for the cancellation of removal attempts (such as medical reasons,\norganisational problems etc.). Since removals are not announced to the persons concerned, it is also\nlikely that persons can simply not be found on the date of the scheduled removals, due to them staying\nat another place rather than because they are deliberately avoiding arrest.1162 Nevertheless, despite the\nlack of empirical evidence, the comparatively high number of cancellations of removal attempts is often\nassociated with the absconding of the persons concerned.1163\n\nStatistics on removals from detention also show that an increase in detention is not necessarily associated\nwith a higher number of removals.1164 In addition, there are strong differences between the Federal States\nin how often detention actually results in a removal: by way of example, in North Rhine Westphalia and\nRhineland Palatinate, four out of five people detained in the first half of 2023 were also removed, while\nthe ration is only one out of ten in Saxony.1165 Nonetheless, over the past years, requests for a more\nfrequent use of detention pending removal in the political debate resulted in several legislative reforms\nsince 2015, of which the ones adopted over the past four years are briefly presented here.1166 August\n2019 saw the entry into force of the Second Act for an improved enforcement of the obligation to leave\nthe country (Zweites Gesetz zur besseren Durchsetzung der Ausreisepflicht, also known as the ‘Orderly\nReturn Act’/Geordnete-Rückkehr-Gesetz). The law expanded authorities’ power to access private\napartments and to arrest persons to be removed, expanded the grounds for detention and introduced a\nnew form of ‘detention to enforce the obligation to cooperate’ with authorities (Mitwirkungshaft. Section\n62 VI Residence Act; for details see the 2019 to the AIDA Country Report for Germany).1167\n\nThe numerous and increasingly restrictive legal changes in previous years continued with a new detention\nprovision in Section 62c Residence Act adopted in November 2020 and an extension of detention\npossibilities for criminal offenders which entered into force on 31 December 2022 (see Grounds for\ndetention). In October 2023, the Federal Government issued a proposal for an additional reform of\ndetention and the return procedure in the Act on the Improvement of Removals\n(Rückführungsverbesserungsgesetz).1168 The bill, which inter alia would expand detention grounds and\nduration, was voted on in parliament on 18 January 2024 but has not entered into force as of end February\n2024 (see Legal framework of detention).1169\n\n\n\n\n1162\n       Brief analysis of Dr. Thomas Hohlfeld (assistant to the parliamentary group of The Left) of the Federal\n       Governments reply 19/17100, 20 March 2020, available in German at: https://bit.ly/4aqLV7g, 5.\n1163\n       See for example DIE ZEIT; Mehr als jede zweite Abschiebung gescheitert, 24 February 2019, available in\n       German at: http://bit.ly/3wobZ06.\n1164\n       Mediendienst Integration, \"Im großen Stil\" abschieben?, 23 October 2023, available in German at:\n       https://bit.ly/3tPuZqp and Victoria Rietig and Mona Lou Günnewig, Deutsche Rückkehrpolitik und\n       Abschiebungen. It reported that housing shortages prolonged stays in accommodation centres and that the obligation negatively affected labour-market access; the rule had been made permanent in 2019 without the evaluation originally contemplated before extension.", "question_ids": ["5B.7", "5B.8", "6B.2", "6B.3", "8B.7", "8B.8"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "Institutional evaluation published in 2023, assessing a policy operating during the period.", "ruler_attribution": "The evaluation concerns federal policy and BAMF administration; the source does not show that Scholz personally initiated or altered the residence rule.", "limitations": ["The rule predates Scholz's chancellorship, although its continued operation and evaluation fall within the period."], "start_unit": 227, "end_unit": 228, "locator": "page 227 through page 228", "exact_excerpt": "partners or minor children) live in another place.1514 In 2022 the legal framework for the obligatory place\nof residence has been slightly changed. New exception grounds for the obligation to take up a specific\nplace of residence have been introduced. Accordingly, beneficiaries of international protection may be\nexempted from the obligation if their participation in an integration course or other qualification measures\nrequires them to move somewhere else.\n\nAccording to the official explanatory memorandum, the residence rule is supposed to promote sustainable\nintegration by preventing segregation of communities.1515 However, it has been questioned whether the\nway in which the provision has been put into effect is suitable for achieving the intended aim.1516 A study\nby the Technical University of Dresden on existing ‘residence rules’ was published in March 2018. The\nauthor points out that it will take more time to assess the positive or negative effects of the regulations\nintroduced in 2016. At the same time, she concludes that the new measures should not be expected to\nhave too many regulatory effects on the labour and housing markets and on integration efforts of refugees.\nThis is because the number of persons affected by the new regulations was rather low in comparison to\nthe overall migrant and refugee communities in Germany. Furthermore, she argues that integration\nprocesses are generally difficult to regulate by law.1517\n\nA brief analysis of the impact of the residence rule was published in January 2020.1518 This paper is based\non the ‘IAB-BAMF-SOEP survey’, a representative study on the living conditions of refugees which has\nbeen carried out on an annual basis since 2016. In this analysis, the situation of refugees who are subject\nto the residence rule is compared to other refugees, in particular those that were granted refugee status\nat an earlier date, before the introduction of the regulation. The duration of stay in Germany as well as\nother regional and individual factors were taken into account in order to avoid possible distortions. The\nmain findings of this analysis are:\n     v Refugees who are subject to the residence rule are less likely to be employed;\n     v Refugees who are subject to the residence rule are less likely to live in private accommodation\n         (as opposed to collective accommodation);\n     v It could not be ascertained whether the residence rule had a positive or negative impact on\n         refugees’ German language skills or on their (successful) participation in integration courses.\n\nAn independent study from ‘Paritaetischer Gesamtverband’ from 2022 confirmed these findings. In the\nstudy, the obligation has been highly criticised as standing in contrast to the aim of facilitating integration.\nE.g. access to the job market, access to regular housing and protection for victims of violence is heavily\nimpeded by the obligation.1519\n\nIn a ruling of 4 September 2018, the High Administrative Court of North Rhine-Westphalia decided that\nthe Federal State’s regulation on the residence obligation for refugees was illegal. According to the court,\nthe wording of the directive was too restrictive as it stated that refugees ‘should, as a rule’ be obliged to\nreside in the town or district to which they had been accommodated during the asylum procedure.1520\nAlthough the decision was restricted to North Rhine-Westphalia, it highlights that authorities generally\nhave to conduct an individual assessment to determine whether a residence obligation is useful ’to\nenhance the prospects of a sustainable integration’.1521 In the aftermath of the judgment the government\nof North Rhine-Westphalia generally evaluated the states rules and amended those parts where the court\n\n1514\n       Section 12a(5) Residence Act.\n1515\n       Explanatory memorandum, Bundestag Document no. 18/8614, 42-43.\n1516\n       Clara Schlotheuber and Sebastian Röder, Integrative (?) The explanatory memorandum to the integration\nact of 2016 had stated that the decision on whether the rule would be discontinued or extended should\nbe based on an evaluation of its impact. Although this evaluation never took place, a new law was\nintroduced in the spring of 2019 and entered into force on 12 July 2019.1523 This law has now made the\nresidence rule permanent. The main principles of the regulation remain unchanged, as only a few\nclarifications were introduced (e.g. concerning the continuation of the residence rule after an authorised\nmove to another Federal State). Furthermore, a new sanction was introduced for persons who have\nmoved to another place without permission while they were subject to the residence rule: In these cases,\nthe obligation to stay in the assigned place of residence can now be extended ‘by the (same) period of\ntime at which the foreigner has not complied with the obligation’.1524 Again, the explanatory memorandum\nto the law states that an evaluation of the impact of Section 12a of the Residence Act is supposed to take\nplace within three years.1525 With the amendments in the legal framework slightly improve the situation of\nbeneficiaries since more exceptions and reasons for lifting the obligation have been introduced.\n\nHowever, according to an evaluation by the BAMF from 2023, the obligation to take up residence in\nspecific does not have a positive effect on integration. To the contrast, due to the general shortcomings\nin housing, the obligation fosters a prolonged stay in accommodation centres, since refugees cannot find\nprivate housing in the assigned places. Furthermore, the obligation to reside in specific places has a\nnegative impact on the access to the labour market.1526\n\n        2. Travel documents\n\nPersons with refugee status are entitled to ‘travel documents for refugees’ (‘Reiseausweis für\nFlüchtlinge’) in accordance with Article 28 of the 1951 Refugee Convention. The travel document for\nrefugees is either automatically issued together with the residence permit after status determination has\nbecome final, or it is issued upon application. The document shall adhere to European standards1527 and\ntherefore has to include a storage medium with the facial image, fingerprints etc.1528\n\nThe duration of the travel document for refugees is ‘up to three years’. For each renewal the refugee has\nto pay a EUR 70 fee.1529 Alternatively, it can be issued as a preliminary travel document, i.e. without an\nelectronic storage medium, for ‘up to one year’.1530 A prolongation of the document is not possible, so\nrefugees have to apply for a new document once the old one has expired. If their travel document expires\non a journey, they may exceptionally apply for a travel document for aliens (see below) from abroad.1531\nIn these cases applicants need a valid residence permit and the embassy checks whether a cessation of\nthe German residence permit due to an absence of more than six months from Germany can be\n\n\n\n\n1522\n       Ministry for children, family, refugees and notwendigen Maßnahmen ergriffen, von der Wiederinbetriebnahme von Kohlekraftwerken, der Verlängerung der Nutzung der Atomkraftwerke über die Füllung der Gasspeicher und viele Importmöglichkeiten für Gas über westeuropäische Häfen und dann eben neue Terminals an den norddeutschen Küsten. Die ersten drei haben die Schiffe, mit denen das Gas importiert werden kann, gewissermaßen schon gesehen. Das ist ein gutes Zeichen für Deutschland.\nIch will an dieser Stelle sagen, dass das Tempo, das wir hier gezeigt haben, ein Tempo sein muss, das unser ganzes Land prägt, dass wir überall, wo es darum geht, die Zukunft zu gewinnen, mit entsprechender Geschwindigkeit vorankommen müssen. Wenn wir jetzt, wo wir die Krise aktuell bekämpfen mussten, sehen, wie schnell das geht, müssen wir das auch machen, wenn es zum Beispiel darum geht, die erneuerbaren Energien, Windkraft auf hoher See, an Land, Solarenergie und die Stromnetze auszubauen, wenn es darum geht, die Transformation der Industrie zu begleiten, damit tatsächlich gelingt, was wir uns vorgenommen haben: Wir wollen das erste CO2-neutral wirtschaftende Industrieland der Welt sein und als viertgrößte Volkswirtschaft der Welt entsprechend voranmarschieren, um zu zeigen, dass es geht, und anderen Mut zu machen, auch diesen Weg zu beschreiten.\nBei allem haben wir den Zusammenhalt und den Respekt vor den Bürgerinnen und Bürgern nicht vergessen; das zeigen all die Leistungen, die wir auf den Weg gebracht haben.\nUrsache für die Herausforderungen, die wir zu bewältigen hatten, die unsere eigene Volkswirtschaft betreffen, ist der furchtbare imperialistische Angriffskrieg Russlands gegen die Ukraine, ein furchtbarer Krieg, in dem die Grundsätze, auf die wir uns jahrzehntelang in Europa verständigt hatten, infrage gestellt worden sind. Einer dieser Grundsätze lautet: Grenzen dürfen nicht mit Gewalt verschoben werden. Das ist das, was Russland aufgekündigt hat. Das ist die Zeitenwende, von der ich hier in diesem Bundestag gesprochen habe.\nDeshalb ist es richtig, dass wir eng abgestimmt mit unseren internationalen Partnern die Ukraine unterstützen: finanziell, mit humanitären Mitteln, aber eben auch mit Waffenlieferungen. Das haben wir in einem Bruch mit jahrzehntelanger Staatspraxis in Deutschland getan, und mittlerweile kann man sagen: In Europa sind es Großbritannien und wir, die die meiste Unterstützung in Form von Waffen für die Ukraine zur Verfügung stellen. Wenn man fortrechnet, welche Entscheidungen wir schon getroffen haben, kann man sagen: Deutschland wird immer vornean sein, wenn es darum geht, die Ukraine zu unterstützen. Wir sind das Land, das dies mit großer Energie und in einem großen Umfang tut.\nDazu haben wir entschieden, dass wir auch schwere Waffen liefern: die Panzerhaubitze, den Mehrfachraketenwerfer, was Artillerie betrifft. Wir haben den Flakpanzer Gepard zur Verfügung gestellt und tun das weiter sowie ein modernes Luftabwehrsystem wie IRIS-T, für das gerade neue Flugkörper geliefert worden sind. Auch weitere Systeme werden wir liefern. Wir stellen das Patriot-System zur Verfügung, wie ich vor Kurzem zusammen mit der Regierung entschiede habe. Das alles sind Maßnahmen, die dazu beitragen, dass sich die Ukraine verteidigen kann. Alle diese Entscheidungen haben wir im Einklang und in enger Kooperation mit unseren Verbündeten getroffen. Das ist der Grund, warum sie wirksam sind und warum wir sie auch treffen konnten. Das ist das Prinzip, das diese Regierung hat.\nDas werden wir weiter so halten, wie jüngst bei der Entscheidung, zusammen mit den USA Schützenpanzer zu liefern: Bradley die USA und wir unseren Schützenpanzer Marder. Die Franzosen haben zur gleichen Zeit Spähpanzer bereitgestellt. Man sieht: Enge Kooperation und Abstimmung sind das Prinzip – und das ist richtig; denn es ist wirklich Krieg in Europa. Nicht weit weg von uns hier in Berlin findet er statt gegen ein großes Land, die Ukraine. Deshalb müssen wir bei allem, was wir tun, immer klarstellen, dass wir das Notwendige und das Mögliche machen, um die Ukraine zu unterstützen, dass wir aber gleichzeitig eine Eskalation des Krieges zu einem Krieg zwischen Russland und der Nato verhindern. Dieses Prinzip werden wir weiterhin immer beachten.\nDabei gibt es für diese Dinge keine mathematischen Gewissheiten. Keiner kann einem erklären, wo genau die richtigen und die falschen Entscheidungen sind. Und deshalb ist es richtig und mit voller Absicht geschehen, dass wir uns Stück für Stück vorangearbeitet haben. Dieses Prinzip werden wir auch in Zukunft weiterverfolgen. Es ist das einzige Prinzip, das in einer so gefährlichen Angelegenheit Sicherheit auch für Europa und Deutschland gewährleistet.\nDas gilt jetzt auch für die jüngste Entscheidung, die die Bundesregierung getroffen hat: Wir werden der Ukraine auch Kampfpanzer vom Typ Leopard 2 zur Verfügung stellen. Das ist das Ergebnis erneut intensiver Beratungen mit unseren Verbündeten und internationalen Partnern. Ich will ausdrücklich sagen: Es war richtig und es ist richtig, dass wir uns nicht haben treiben lassen, sondern dass wir auf diese enge Kooperation in einer solchen Angelegenheit setzen und sie auch fortsetzen.\nUnser Ziel ist es, rasch zwei Panzerbataillone zusammen mit unseren Verbündeten bereitzustellen. Es gibt viele Länder, die gerne mitliefern wollen, und wir werden das koordinieren und sie einbeziehen, damit das Schritt für Schritt auch möglich wird. Deutschland wird, wie gesagt, Leopard-2A6-Panzer zur Verfügung stellen, die aus den Beständen der Bundeswehr stammen. Wenn sie das weiterhin wollen, werden wir die ukrainischen Besatzungen hier in Deutschland ausbilden. Wir werden Ausbildung, Logistik, Munition und Wartung der Systeme gewährleisten und, wie gesagt, den Partnerländern ermöglichen, dass sie liefern können. Das ist das richtige Prinzip. Es geht hier um sehr wirksame Waffensysteme, und es ist richtig, dass wir diese Waffensysteme niemals alleine, sondern immer in enger Kooperation bereitstellen.\nEin Wort zum Schluss. Es gibt in diesem Land viele Bürgerinnen und Bürger, die sich Sorgen machen, auch angesichts einer solchen Entscheidung und angesichts der Dimension, die diese Waffe mit sich bringt. Und deshalb möchte ich diesen Bürgerinnen und Bürgern hier an dieser Stelle sagen: Vertrauen Sie mir, vertrauen Sie der Bundesregierung. Wir werden, weil wir international abgestimmt handeln, weiter sicherstellen, dass diese Unterstützung möglich ist, ohne dass die Risiken für unser Land darüber in eine falsche Richtung wachsen.\nDas ist der Grund, warum wir das so tun, und so werden wir es auch weiter machen.\nBeitrag teilen\nper E-Mail teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz\nper Facebook teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz\nper Threema teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz\nBeitrag teilen\nE-Mail\nper E-Mail teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz\nFacebook\nper Facebook teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz\nThreema\nper Threema teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz\nWhatsApp\nper Whatsapp teilen, Rede von Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz", "excerpt_sha256": "d730cd3250c372ddfec443ea0b270ac553781760dac73a18be252972707a9769", "verification_status": "corrected", "verification_notes": ["Training of Ukrainian crews was stated conditionally, so the summary was narrowed to Germany providing training generally."]}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0016-E005", "source_id": "SRC-ffa5a98c7207485e", "url": "https://www.bundesregierung.de/breg-en/service/archive/speech-by-chancellor-scholz-at-the-munich-security-conference-on-17-february-2023-in-munich-2166536", "title": "Speech by Chancellor Scholz at the Munich Security Conference on 17 February 2023 in Munich", "publisher": "Federal Government of Germany", "raw_sha256": "8277a801e1e91a012f5b0e3e13d617f0f1c91da6b650d0c9d4c462d11f339657", "fact_summary": "Scholz said Germany would balance comprehensive, prolonged support for Ukraine with avoiding unintended escalation, coordinate key steps closely among allies, and maintain caution over hasty unilateral action; he linked this approach to successive deliveries of artillery, air defence, armoured vehicles, Patriot systems, and battle tanks.", "question_ids": ["1B.2", "1B.8", "2B.5", "2B.7", "8B.5", "8B.9"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "17 February 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "Scholz stated the policy and described decisions by the government he led.", "limitations": ["This is a stated crisis-management framework, not independent evidence of effectiveness or absence of escalation."], "start_unit": 6, "end_unit": 7, "locator": "extracted HTML block 6 through extracted HTML block 7", "exact_excerpt": "The Ukrainians are defending their freedom at such high cost and with remarkably impressive resolve.\nAnd we’re supporting them – as comprehensively and as long as is necessary.\nGermany alone provided more than 12 billion euro to assist Ukraine last year.\nWe’ve taken in more than one million Ukrainian refugees – granting them full access to our labour market, our schools, our universities.\nWe’re supplying cutting-edge weapons, ammunition and other military goods – more than any other country in continental Europe.\nThat not only meets the – it has to be said, justified – expectations of our partners and Allies.\nWe’re also assuming the responsibility which a country of Germany’s size, location and economic clout has to shoulder in times such as these.\nIn doing this, we have broken with decades-long principles of German policy. For example, the maxim that we don’t supply weapons in a war such as this.\nI understand if some people here in Germany are concerned and question our decisions.\nI want to say to them that it’s not our arms supplies which are prolonging the war.\nThe opposite is true – and that brings me to my second point: the sooner President Putin realises that he cannot achieve his imperialist objective, the greater the chance that the war will end soon with a withdrawal of Russia’s occupying forces.\nThat’s also Ukraine’s aim – as President Zelensky emphasised at our meetings last week in Paris and Brussels and once again just now.\nWe – Europe as well as the transatlantic and international communities – are firmly united in pursuit of this goal.\nBy the way, this includes documenting and prosecuting war crimes and crimes against humanity committed by Russians in Ukraine.\nIt’s good that the Munich Security Conference is addressing this issue, for there can be no lasting peace without justice.\nAt the same time, we’re making sure that war doesn’t break out between NATO and Russia.\nMy third message is therefore: we will continue to strike a balance between providing the best possible support for Ukraine and avoiding an unintended escalation.\nAnd I’m pleased and grateful that President Biden and many other Allies share this view.\nAfter all, the path we’ve embarked upon together runs through uncharted territory.\nFor the first time in our history, a nuclear power is waging an imperialist war of aggression here on European soil.\nThere’s no blueprint for what needs to be done in this situation.\nI believe we would do well to carefully weigh up all the consequences of our actions and closely coordinate all key steps among the Allies.\nFor this is a war in our neighbourhood, in Europe – a dangerous war.\nAnd despite all the pressure to take action, which undoubtedly exists:\nin this key question, caution must take priority over hasty decisions, unity over solo actions.\n\nAnd from the outset, we have to provide our support in such a way that it can be continued for a prolonged period.\nTo date, any decision we’ve made on supplying new weapons systems has been taken on that premise: in the case of howitzers and multiple launch rocket systems, in the case of air defence weapons, armoured infantry fighting vehicles, Patriot missile batteries and, most recently, Western battle tanks.\nAnd we will continue to decide on that basis in future.\nThat also means that all those who can supply battle tanks of this kind should now actually do so. Defence Minister Pistorius, Foreign Minister Baerbock and myself are canvassing intensively for this – and we will do so again here in Munich.\nGermany will do everything it can to make this decision easier for our partners. For instance, by training Ukrainian soldiers here in Germany or providing support in terms of supplies and logistics.\nBy the way, I see this as an example of the kind of leadership which everyone is entitled to expect from Germany – and I expressly offer it to our friends and partners.\nAnd that brings me to my fourth message: Germany is committed to living up to its responsibility for Europe’s security and that of NATO Allied territory – without any ifs or buts.\nIn previous years, it has often been said from this platform that Germany has to live up to its responsibility in the security policy sphere.\nI share this view – and we will indeed fulfil our responsibility.\nBy providing an additional brigade to protect Lithuania.\nBy assisting Poland and Slovakia with air defence and air policing.\nBy protecting critical infrastructure in the North and Baltic Seas.\nAnd by leading NATO’s Very High Readiness Joint Task Force and keeping 17,000 troops on standby to that end.\nIn order to be able to accomplish that – and more in future – we are putting an end to the neglect of the Bundeswehr.\nWe’ve laid the foundation for this by creating the special fund of 100 billion euro for our armed forces.\nTo realise this, we amended the Basic Law – with the support of our country’s largest opposition party.\nThis fund allows us to finally change gear in terms of building up our Bundeswehr’s capabilities.\nOf course, the new combat aircraft, helicopters, ships and tanks will result in an increase in expenditure on ammunition and equipment, maintenance, exercises, training and personnel.\nAnd I therefore want to take this opportunity to reaffirm what I said in the Bundestag three days after the war began:\nGermany will increase its defence expenditure to two percent of gross domestic product on a permanent basis.\nIn order to invest this funding in a meaningful and sustainable manner, we need a high-performing and competitive arms industry – in Germany and in Europe as a whole.\nTherefore, my fifth point is: the European Union must pull together strategically when it comes to arms policy.\nWe’re developing the Future Combat Air System (FCAS) together with France and Spain and the Main Ground Combat System (MGCS) with France.\nAnd we’re also making progress in the joint development of European capabilities.\nOne example of this is the European Sky Shield Initiative proposed by Germany, which is intended to strengthen Europe’s air defence within the NATO framework.", "excerpt_sha256": "207090b677ad618c66c7e769a7e753d62b4ccc3d38fed2b6a4a27c64be26fdb5", Verkehrsminister die Klimaziele nicht erreiche, wäre es angebrachter, den Minister auszuwechseln oder ein Tempolimit einzuführen als das Klimaschutzgesetz aufzuweichen. Scholz lobte hingegen Volker Wissing als „sehr, sehr guten Verkehrsminister“. Er sei „richtig unterwegs“, wenn es darum gehe, das Kernnetz der Eisenbahn voranzubringen. Für den Investitionsbedarf im Verkehr müssten in den nächsten Jahren zusätzliche 45 Milliarden Euro mobilisiert werden. Auf die Lkw-Maut werde es einen CO\n2\n-Aufschlag geben.\nAuch dem\nFDP-Abgeordneten Valentin Abel\nbestätigte der Kanzler, dass die Instandsetzung des Kernnetzes der Bahn eine Herausforderung der nächsten zehn Jahre sein werde. Gleiches gelte auch für Ausbauvorhaben. Von Abel auf die Nutzung synthetischer Kraftstoffe, sogenannter\nE-Fuels\nangesprochen, erklärte Scholz, diese spielten für Schiffe, im Luftverkehr und für schwere Transportaufgaben eine große Rolle. Es solle möglich sein, ab 2035 auch Fahrzeuge mit\nE-Fuels\nzu betanken. Die Dimension dieser Möglichkeit könne er allerdings nicht ermessen, so der Kanzler. Gegenüber der\nFDP-Abgeordneten Carina Konrad\nwies Scholz in diesem Zusammenhang auf die Notwendigkeit der Beschleunigung hin. CO\n2\n-neutrale Mobilität werde gebraucht.\nFachkräftemangel und Kindergrundsicherung\nNatalie Pawlik (SPD)\nerkundigte sich nach den Prioritäten angesichts des Fachkräftemangels in der Pflege, im Handwerk und in öffentlichen Verwaltungen. Scholz sagte, zunächst gelte es, die Potenziale im eigenen Land zu nutzen, Weiterbildung zu ermöglichen und Frauen eine Berufstätigkeit zu erleichtern. Das werde aber nicht reichen, und mit dem angekündigten Fachkräfte-Einwanderungsgesetz werde man dafür sorgen, bürokratische Hürden für die Zuwanderung von Fachkräften aus der ganzen Welt abzubauen.\nEinige Fragen betrafen das Projekt der Kindergrundsicherung. Der\nCDU-Abgeordneten Silvia Breher\nteilte Scholz mit, Kindergeld und Kinderzuschlag seien bereits angehoben worden. Während das Kindergeld zu 100 Prozent in Anspruch genommen werde, liege die Quote beim Kinderzuschlag für einkommensärmere Familien nur bei etwa 30 Prozent. Es müsse daher dafür gesorgt werden, diesen Anteil zu erhöhen.\nNina Stahr (Bündnis 90/Die Grünen)\nfragte nach den zentralen Instrumenten im Kampf gegen Kinderarmut. Scholz nannte hier die Förderung der Berufstätigkeit der Eltern, aber auch die Angebotsstruktur für die Kinderbetreuung. Die Arbeitslosigkeit sei dort hoch, wo Schul- und Berufsabschlüsse fehlen. Gute Bildung sei ein humanistischer Anspruch, so der Kanzler. Jeder solle ein einzigartiges Lebensmodell entwickeln können.\nZuwanderung und Kanzleramtserweiterungsbau\nDie\nCSU-Abgeordnete Andrea Lindholz\n\nwies auf das gestiegene Ausmaß der irregulären Einwanderung hin. Die Regierung lasse Schreiben der Kommunen unbeantwortet. Scholz sagte, die Deutschen hätten rund eine Million Menschen aus der Ukraine aufgenommen. 2022 seien dafür 3,5 Milliarden Euro bereitgestellt worden, fünf Milliarden Euro jährlich koste deren Aufnahme in die Bürgergeld-Regelungen. Mit Herkunftsstaaten würden Migrationspartnerschaften abgeschlossen, damit diese Migranten zurücknehmen, deren Asylverfahren abgeschlossen ist.\nLindholz verwies auf Georgien und die Maghreb-Staaten, die als sichere Herkunftsstaaten eingestuft werden sollten, was an den Grünen scheitere. Scholz sagte, es werde im Hinblick auf diese Länder von Fall zu Fall entschieden. Erforderlich sei, die Ausländerbehörden zu digitalisieren.\nDr. Gesine Lötzsch (Die Linke)\nsprach den geplanten Erweiterungsbau des Kanzleramtes mit Kosten von 770,2 Millionen Euro an und nannte dies den „Neubau des zweiten Kanzleramtes“, der\nAfD-Abgeordnete Marcus Bühl\nbezeichnete ihn als „Prunkbau“, was der Kanzler bestritt. Damit werde den Architekten unrecht getan.\nWaffenrecht und globaler Süden\nDie von Innenministerin Faeser geplante Novelle des Waffenrechts interessierte\nDunja Kreiser (SPD)\n. Es gehe darum, so Scholz, die rechtlichen Rahmenbedingungen zu präzisieren, damit die Verwaltung Entscheidungen treffen kann. Über eine Differenzierung zwischen illegalen und legalen Waffen werde diskutiert, in der Verwaltungspraxis sei dies nicht einfach.\nMarcel Emmerich (Bündnis 90/Die Grünen)\nwies auf Ermittlungen in der Reichsbürgerszene hin, die Verfassungsfeinde besäßen Schusswaffen. Scholz sagte, das Waffenrecht solle so streng gefasst werden, dass es den Sicherheitsanforderungen der Bürger entspricht.\nDeborah Dürin\ng (Bündnis 90/Die\nGrünen)\nfragte nach der Entwicklungszusammenarbeit mit dem globalen Süden, um die Resilienz dieser Staaten zu fördern. Scholz sagte, Deutschland werde seiner Verantwortung der Welt gegenüber gerecht werden. Es werde eine Herausforderung sein, gemachte Zusagen einzuhalten, aber man wolle sich davor nicht drücken. (vom/29.03.2023)\nReden zu diesem Tagesordnungspunkt\nBildinformationen\nBärbel Bas\n© Bärbel Bas / Photothek Media Lab\nBas, Bärbel\nBundestagspräsidentin\n()\nBildinformationen\nOlaf Scholz",
